## Page 126

6. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Hā Mīm (H.M.):**

This set of Qur’anic initials occurs in seven Meccan suras which are recorded consecutively (40 through 46), and were revealed consecutively (60 through 66). These seven suras are Ghāfir (Forgiver), Fussilat (Elucidated), Al-Shūrā (Consensus), Al-Zukhruf (Vanities), Al-Dukhān (Air Pollution), Al-Jāthiya (Amenability), and Al-Ahqāf (The Dunes). One of these suras, namely, Al-Shūrā (number 42), contains a second set of initials, namely, ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf (‘A.S.Q.), and will be studied in further detail later.

The computer printouts of the percentage values of Hā Mīm throughout the Qur’an, the absolute frequency values, and the average per verse values are shown in Tables 34, 35, and 36. The three values for each of the seven Hā Mīm suras have been grouped together and averaged (Table 37). When we examine the list of percentage values we note that the seven Hā Mīm suras are scattered throughout the list. Obviously, not all the Hā Mīm suras are expected to exhibit mathematical superiority with regard to the letters Hā Mīm. What remains to be seen, therefore, is whether or not their average values exhibit any special mathematical patterns. The average percentage of Hā Mīm in the seven suras is 10.095 (Table 37). When we insert this average value in the percentage list we find that 33 out of the 114 suras have a higher percentage of Hā Mīm. Computation of the magnitudes of difference and the net overall frequency value of Hā Mīm were carried out for these

## Page 127

33 suras. The results are shown in Table 38. Examination of this table leads to several interesting observations. Thus, we note that five suras have plus signs, i.e., they are superior to the united Hā Mīm suras in the net overall frequency of the letters Hā Mīm. These five suras are Hūd (Prophet Hūd), Al-Fatḥ (Victory), Al-Mumtaḥina (The Test), Al-Rūm (The Romans), and Al-Jumu'a (Friday). Two of these are Meccan suras, and three are Medinan. But we note that the two Meccan suras (Hūd & Al-Rūm) are initialed, and that each one of them belongs to a group of suras which have identical initials. Thus, just as we grouped and averaged the Hā Mīm suras, it appears that we have to group and average the other sister suras as well. It should be pointed out that the only Qur’anic initials which exist in consecutive suras are Hā Mīm, Alif Lām Mīm, and Alif Lām Rā. We see here that each one of these groups is represented, and required to be grouped and averaged. Thus, the Alif Lām Mīm group is represented by sura Al-Rūm, and the Alif Lām Rā group is represented by sura Hūd (see Table 38). We note here that the member suras in each one of these three groups are mathematically dependent on each other, if we are to observe any specific pattern. This fact provides us with one possible explanation for having identical initials prefixing certain suras. The Qur’anic initials, therefore, were not placed in their respective suras randomly. In this study of the initials Hā Mīm, we find it necessary to group and average the seven Hā Mīm suras, the six Alif Lām Mīm suras, and

## Page 128

the five Alif Lām Rā suras. The average percentage of occurrence of Hā Mīm in each of these three groups is shown in Table 39. Having done this, we note that **THERE IS NOT ONE SINGLE SURA IN THE WHOLE QUR’AN WHERE ALL THREE VALUES OF HĀ MĪM ARE HIGHER THAN THEIR COUNTERPARTS IN THE HĀ MĪM SURAS.**

We also note that if we exclude the average per verse values, the overall frequency values of the 33 suras analyzed in Table 38 are found lower than the overall frequency of Hā Mīm in the Hā Mīm suras. And, if we take all three values into consideration (i.e., including the average per verse value), we find that the ONLY suras which are superior to the Hā Mīm suras in the net overall frequency of Hā Mīm are Medinan suras. As shown in Table 38, these are the three Medinan suras Al-Fatḥ (no. 48), Al-Mum-tahina (no. 60), and Al-Jumu‘a (no. 62). Thus, we note here that the unified Hā Mīm suras are **SUPERIOR TO ALL THE MECCAN SURAS** in the overall frequency of the letters Hā Mīm. The reader is reminded that the seven Hā Mīm suras are Meccan. This mathematical feature, therefore, contributes to the confirmation of the location of revelation, just as we saw with the Ṭā Hā Qur’anic initials (page 96).

## Page 134

7. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS **Ṭā Sīn (Ṭ.S.)**:

and

8. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS **Ṭā Sīn Mīm (Ṭ.S.M.)**

This group of initials occurs in three suras which are consecutive both in the recording sequence and the chronological revelation sequence of Qur’an. Two suras are initialed with the three letters Ṭā Sīn Mīm. These are Al-Shu‘arā’ (Poets) and Al-Qasas (History). The Qur’anic initials constitute an independent verse in these two suras. The two letters Ṭā Sīn occur in sura Al-Naml (The Ant), and they make up only part of the first verse, rather than an independent verse. The recording numbers of suras Al-Shu‘arā’ (Poets), Al-Naml (The Ant), and Al-Qasas (History) are 26, 27, and 28, respectively. The revelation numbers of these three suras are 47, 48, and 49, respectively. Thus, we have here three consecutive suras where the two-lettered set of Qur’anic initials separates the three-lettered sets of initials.

There have been many discussions and speculations concerning the absence of Mīm from the Qur’anic initials of the middle sura, Al-Naml. The discussion presented by ‘Abdullah Yūsuf ‘Ali, and shown on page 27 of this book, is typical, and provides the reader with a good example of the literature treatment of this subject. Most books of tafsīr present similar theories regarding this Ṭā Sīn Mīm/Ṭā Sīn/Ṭā Sīn Mīm arrangement.

## Page 135

The computer printouts of the percentage values of Ṭā Sīn and Ṭā Sīn Mīm, their absolute frequency of occurrence values, and their average per verse values throughout the Qur’an are shown in Tables 40 through 45. When we look at the ascending order of percentage values of Ṭā Sīn Mīm (Table 40), we note that sura Al-Qasas (History) ranks 36, and sura Al-Shu‘arā’ (Poets) ranks 79, out of the Qur’an’s 114 suras. It takes little thinking to realize that exactly 35 suras have a higher percentage of Ṭā Sīn Mīm than that of sura Al-Shu‘arā’, and exactly 35 suras have a lower percentage of Ṭā Sīn Mīm than sura Al-Qasas. Figure 3 clarifies this observation. The arrangement of the three Ṭā Sīn/Ṭā Sīn Mīm suras, both in revelation order and recording order, gives a special sense of balance, as can be seen in Figure 3. The observation that 35 suras contain a higher proportion of Ṭā Sīn Mīm than sura Al-Shu‘arā’, and the same number of suras contain a lower percentage than sura Al-Qasas, leaves little doubt that the omission of Mīm in sura Al-Naml’s Qur’anic initials was not coincidental. Let us also remember that the two Ṭā Sīn Mīm sets of initials form an independent verse each, while the Ṭā Sīn set which separates them does not make up an independent verse.

The fact that this mathematical balance, manifested through the use of electronic computers, depends on the relative frequency of occurrence of Ṭā Sīn Mīm throughout the Qur’an is an undeniable evidence that the Qur’an could never be man-made, let alone man-

## Page 137

made by an illiterate man 14 centuries ago when the concepts of “percentage” and “proportion” were not known. In order to appreciate this miraculous phenomenon, let us go back in time 1400 years. A sizeable book is being constructed over a period of 23 years. The component sentences are widely separated from each other both in time and place. In advance, Someone knew the exact *relative* frequency of the letters Ṭā (Ṭ), Sīn (S), and Mīm (M) (we have expressed this relative frequency by the ‘percent’ concept which was unknown in those days). Thus, that “Someone” knew that out of 114 chapters, exactly 35 will have a higher relative frequency of Ṭā Sīn Mīm than chapter X, and exactly 35 will have a lower relative frequency than chapter Z. To indicate this fact, chapters X and Z were “tagged” with the letters Ṭā Sīn Mīm. And to convey the sense of balance inherent in this observation, chapter Y was “tagged” with the less powerful initials Ṭā Sīn (only 2 initials, and only part of the verse. See Figure 3). Obviously, this kind of advance knowledge, even if the book was being written within one week rather than 23 years and this kind of control over the distribution of individual alphabets, are unattainable by man.

In spite of the remoteness of the possibility of coincidence, some critics may actually claim that this 35-above-and-35-below arrangement is coincidental. Just in case this kind of criticism may arise, God Almighty has provided us with one additional observation which rules out the possibility of coincidence. Thus, when the reader

## Page 138

looks up the list of absolute frequency of occurrence of Țā Sīn Mīm (Table 41), he will note that sura Al-Shu‘arā’ (XXVI) contains 615 Țā Sīn Mīm letters, while sura Al-Qasas (XXVIII) contains 580. The difference between the two suras is 615 – 580 = 35. This striking observation needs no further comment.

The calculations and arrangement of information from the suras were all carried out by the computer, away from human bias. It would take the reader less than five minutes to count the number of suras above and/or below suras Al-Shu‘arā’ and/or Al-Qasas. It would take less than half an hour to count the letters Țā, Sīn, and Mīm in these two suras, and discover for one’s self that the difference between them is exactly 35. Thus, the evidence that THE QUR’AN IS THE WORD OF GOD; the evidence that THE QUR’AN CAN NEVER BE MAN-MADE, is physical, touchable, and examinable evidence.

The mathematical phenomena we just observed, are but one facet of the multi-dimensional miracle of the Qur’anic initials Țā Sīn/Țā Sīn Mīm. Thus, when we look at the list of percentage values of Țā Sīn, we note that sura Al-Naml (The Ant) which is initialized by Țā Sīn contains 2.533% of the letters Țā Sīn. To find out whether the suras with a higher percentage are genuinely superior in the overall frequency of Țā Sīn, the net overall frequency was computed as described on page 68. The results are shown in Table 46, and they reveal that only 3 suras have a higher overall frequency of Țā Sīn than

## Page 139

sura Al-Naml. The first among these three suras is sura Yūsuf (Joseph). But we note that this is an initialed sura which belongs to the Alif Lām Rā (ALR) group. When this sura is averaged with its sister suras as explained previously (page 72), the average percentage value of Ṭā Sīn falls below that of sura Al-Naml. The second sura among the three suras with higher percentage is Al-Isrā’ (The Night Journey). This Meccan sura exhibits a plus sign in front of the net overall frequency value of Ṭā Sīn (Table 46). This means that sura Al-Isrā’ is unquestionably superior to sura Al-Naml with regard to the Qur’anic initials Ṭā Sīn. The third sura with a higher percentage is sura Ṭā Hā. But this sura bears the initial Ṭā in common with sura Al-Naml. Therefore, the superiority of Ṭā Hā is nullified, since it is expected to, and does, have a high overall frequency in the letter Ṭā. This leaves sura Al-Isrā’ as the only sura with a higher overall frequency than sura Al-Naml, with regard to the letters Ṭā Sīn. The final conclusion from this analysis is that **SURA AL-NAML IS SUPERIOR TO ALL SURAS OF THE QUR’AN IN THE OVERALL FREQUENCY OF THE LETTERS ṬĀ SĪN, WITH THE EXCEPTION OF SURA AL-ISRĀ’**.

This observation is quite significant, when we study the revelation position of sura Al-Isrā’. In the revelation sequence of Qur’an, we find that sura Al-Isrā’ falls between the Ṭā Sīn group of suras, and the Alif Lām Rā group (see Table 3). As we shall see later, sura Al-Isrā’ is also superior in the letters Alif Lām Rā, even over

## Page 140

the Alif Lām Rā suras themselves. Only three of the five Alif Lām Rā suras are recorded consecutively in a manner identical to that of the Ṭā Sīn group. One cannot help but observe the underlying sense of balance in these arrangements. Thus, we have three Ṭā Sīn suras which were revealed consecutively, and recorded consecutively. These three suras are Al-Shu‘arā’, Al-Naml, and Al-Qasas. And we have three Alif Lām Rā suras which were revealed consecutively, and recorded consecutively. These suras are Yūnus, Hūd, and Yūsuf. These six suras which have in common the characteristic of being revealed and recorded consecutively are separated from each other by one sura, namely, Al-Isrā’. And it is the only sura which shows a higher frequency of Ṭā Sīn over the preceding group, and also a higher overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā over the succeeding suras. Can these factual observations be mere coincidence? The data herein deems a coincidence highly improbable. Increasingly, we are SEEING that every single word, indeed every single letter in the Qur’ān is carefully calculated.

As for the Ṭā Sīn Mīm suras, we have seen that 35 suras contain a higher percentage of Ṭā Sīn Mīm than sura Al-Shu‘arā’. When we calculate the magnitudes of difference in these 35 suras above and/or below sura Al-Shu‘arā’ (as described on page 68), we find that only 8 suras are actually higher than Al-Shu‘arā’ in the overall frequency of Ṭā Sīn Mīm. These suras are Al-Naml (The Ant), Hūd (Prophet Hūd), Ibrāhīm (Abraham), Muhammad, Al-Rūm (The Romans), Al-Fatḥ (Victory).

## Page 141

Al-Zukhruf (Vanities), and Al-Jumu'a (Friday). Since sura Al-Naml is a member of the Țā Sīn group, it should be taken out of the comparison. This leaves seven suras with a higher overall frequency of Țā Sīn Mīm. Within these seven suras, we find all the multi-sura Qur'anic initials represented. Thus, the Alif Lām Rā group of suras is represented by suras Hūd and Ibrāhīm, the Alif Lām Mīm suras are represented by al-Rūm, and the seven Ḥā Mīm suras are represented by Al-Zukhruf (see Table 47). As shown in Table 48, when these four suras are joined with their respective sister suras, the average percentage of Țā Sīn Mīm is found clearly below that of sura Al-Shu‘arā’. This leaves only three superior suras in the overall frequency of Țā Sīn Mīm. These are suras Muḥammad, Al-Fatḥ, and Al-Jumu'a. And we note that all three suras are Medinan. The final observation, therefore, is that the Țā Sīn Mīm-initialized sura Al-Shu‘arā’, which is a Meccan sura, is superior to ALL MECCAN SURAS in the overall frequency of Țā Sīn Mīm. Even without combining the sister suras which contain identical initials, and without doing any further computations, the three computer printouts of the Țā Sīn Mīm values show that there is not one single sura in the whole Qur’an where all three values of Țā Sīn Mīm are higher than their counterparts in sura Al-Shu‘arā’. One additional observation is the fact that if we disregard the average per verse value (Table 47), we find that sura Al-Shu‘arā’ has the highest overall frequency of Țā Sīn Mīm over all the suras, Meccan and quency

## Page 142

Medinan.

Sura Al-Qasaṣ (History), on the other hand, cannot be mathematically superior in the frequency of Tā Sīn Mīm, since it has the low rank of 36 in the percentage list. It is possible that the fact that sura Al-Qasaṣ balances sura Al-Shu‘arā’ (having 35 suras with a lower relative frequency as compared to 35 suras above sura Al-Shu‘arā’) is the sole mathematical phenomenon of sura Al-Qasaṣ. To find out whether this is true or not, will require vast computations of the overall frequency values of sura Al-Qasaṣ by itself and in combination with sura Al-Shu‘arā’. Since the balance function of sura Al-Qasaṣ, as illustrated in Figure 3, is quite sufficient for our purposes at this time, we will forsake such computations. This book by no means covers all the mathematical phenomena of the Qur’anic initials. It merely scratches the surface.

## Page 152

9. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lām Mīm (A.L.M.):**

These three letters occur as initials in six suras. Four of these suras are Meccan and two are Medinan. The four Meccan suras are Al-‘Ankabūt (The Spider), Al-Rūm (The Romans) Luqmān (Prophet Luqmān), and Al-Sajda (Prostration). The two Medinan suras are Al-Baqara (The Heifer) and Āli-‘Imrān (The ‘Imrān Family). The computer printouts of the percentage values, the absolute frequency values, and the average per verse values of Alif Lām Mīm are shown in Tables 49, 50 and 51, respectively. When we look at the percentage list, we note that the six Alif Lām Mīm suras are scattered among the Qur’ān’s 114 suras. Some kind of combining and averaging of the Alif Lām Mīm sister-suras is clearly required. The average percentage value of Alif Lām Mīm in the six suras is 38.049. As shown in Table 52, 38 suras in the Qur’an contain a higher percentage of Alif Lām Mīm than the average percentage in the six Alif Lām Mīm suras. To compute the overall frequency value of Alif Lām Mīm in these 38 suras, the magnitudes of difference above and or below the average of the six suras were calculated. The results are shown in Table 52. From this table we note that only two Medinan suras exhibit a higher frequency of Alif Lām Mīm than the united six suras. The observation here is that the average overall frequency of the six Alif Lām Mīm suras is SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS, and not to all the Medinan suras. This observation leads to the logical step of checking the 4

## Page 153

Meccan suras by themselves, and the 2 Medinan suras by themselves. The results for the united and averaged 4 Meccan suras are shown in Table 53. From this table we note that all the suras which contain a higher frequency of Alif Lām Mīm than the Meccan Alif Lām Mīm suras are Medinan suras. These are sura Al-Ḥadīd (Iron), Al-Mumtahina (The Test), Al-Nisā’ (Women), Al-Baqara (The Heifer), Āli-‘Imrān (The ‘Imrān Family), Al-Mujādala (The Argument), Al-Aḥzāb (The Allies), Al-Ra‘ad (Thunder), Muḥammad, and Al-Jumu‘a (Friday). All these are Medinan suras (see Tables 2 and 3). The only other suras which show a plus sign in the overall frequency value of Alif Lām Mīm (Table 53), are Ibrāhīm (Abraham) and Yūnus (Jonah). These two suras, however, cannot be included in this particular comparison, since they both belong to the Alif Lām Rā group of suras. They are themselves initialed with Alif and Lām, in common with the Alif Lām Mīm suras being discussed here. Therefore, the mathematical observation pertaining to the Alif Lām Mīm suras is that the averaged four Meccan suras are SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS IN THE OVERALL FREQUENCY OF ALIF LĀM MĪM. We note here that the 4 Meccan suras bearing the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm had to be treated as if they were one continuous sura. Their frequency values of Alif Lām Mīm were grouped together and averaged. We note also that these four suras are recorded in the Qur’an consecutively (29-32), though they were not revealed in consecutive

## Page 154

order (see Tables 2 and 3). We shall observe the same phenomenon with the two Alif Lām Mīm suras which were revealed in Medina, namely, Al-Baqara (The Heifer) and Āli-‘Imrān (The ‘Imrān Family). This is an observation of extreme importance and will be discussed in more detail later.

The average percentage frequency of Alif Lām Mīm in the two Medinan suras (Al-Baqara and Āli-‘Imrān) is 38.556. When we insert this value in the proper place in Table 49, we find that 30 suras contain a higher percentage of Alif Lām Mīm than the two united suras. However, computation of the overall frequency values, as shown in Table 54, reveals that the two Alif Lām Mīm suras are actually SUPERIOR TO ALL SURAS OF THE QUR’AN.

It should be noted that if we treat these two suras separately, we find that the superiority of sura Āli-‘Imrān is surpassed by sura Al-Mā-eda (The Table). Thus, superiority requires that the two Medinan Alif Lām Mīm suras should be treated as if they were one continuous sura. As mentioned above, these two suras are recorded in the Qur’ān consecutively (2 and 3), but they were not revealed in consecutive order (87 and 89). The Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm, therefore, indicate to us that the Alif Lām Mīm suras which are recorded in the Qur’ān in consecutive order must be treated as if they were one continuous sura, regardless of their chronological revelation sequence. These Qur’anic initials, therefore, confirm the recorded sequence of suras

## Page 155

as we know it today. The importance of this observation will become obvious when we study the Alif Lām Rā suras. We shall see that while the Alif Lām Mīm group confirms the recorded sequence of Qur’an, the Alif Lām Rā group confirms the revelation sequence.

## Page 162

10. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS** Alif Lām Rā (A.L.R.):

These letters occur in five suras, Yūunus (Jonah), Hūd (Prophet Hūd), Yūusuf (Joseph), Ibrāhīm (Abraham), and Al-Ḥijr (The Ḥijr Valley). The recorded numbers of these suras are 10, 11, 12, 14, and 15, respectively. Their numbers in the chronological sequence of revelation are 51, 52, 53, 72, and 54, respectively. In the recorded sequence of Qur’an, we note that the sequence is interrupted by sura Al-Ra‘ad (Number 13). And, in the chronological sequence of revelation, the order is interrupted by sura Ibrāhīm (number 72).

The percentage values of Alif Lām Rā, their absolute frequency values, and their average occurrence per verse throughout the Qur’an are shown in Tables 55, 56, and 57, respectively. When we look at the percentage list (Table 55), we note that the five Alif Lām Rā suras are scattered throughout the list. Obviously, they cannot exhibit mathematical superiority with regard to the frequency of Alif Lām Rā on individual basis. Some kind of averaging would be required in order to see consistent, meaningful observations. Thus, the following combinations were studied in detail.

1. All five Alif Lām Rā suras were combined and averaged, i.e., treated as if they were one continuous sura.

2. The four Alif Lām Rā suras which were revealed consecutively were combined and averaged. These suras are Yūunus (Jonah), Hūd, Yūusuf (Joseph), and Al-Ḥijr (The Ḥijr Valley).

## Page 163

3. Sura Ibrāhīm (Abraham) which was revealed separately from the other Alif Lām Rā suras was studied by itself.

4. The Alif Lām Rā suras which are recorded consecutively in the Qur’an were combined together and averaged. These included two groups. One group consisted of suras Yūnus, Hūd, and Yūsuf, whose recorded numbers are 10, 11, and 12. The other group consisted of suras Ibrāhīm and Al-Hijr, whose recorded numbers are 14 and 15.

The overall frequency values of the suras which contained higher percentages of Alif Lām Rā were computed as previously detailed, and compared with the various averages of Alif Lām Rā suras. Due to the vast number of calculations and tabulations involved, only the final observations are reported here. The following mathematical phenomena were observed:

1. When all five Alif Lām Rā suras were treated as if they were one continuous sura, their average was found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS, EXCEPT AL-ISRĀ’ (The Night Journey, no. 17).

2. The same conclusion was obtained when the four consecutively-revealed suras were combined. This meant the exclusion of sura Ibrāhīm, which was revealed some 18 suras after its 4 sister suras.

3. When sura Ibrāhīm was considered by itself, it was also found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS EXCEPT SURA AL-ISRĀ’ (no. 17), with regard to the overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā.

## Page 164

4. When the three suras revealed consecutively, and also recorded consecutively, were combined, their average overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā was found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS WITHOUT EXCEPTION.

5. When the two suras recorded consecutively, but not revealed consecutively (Ibrāhīm and Al-Ḥijr) were combined, their average overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā was found SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS EXCEPT SURA AL-ISRĀ’.

6. When sura Al-Ḥijr was studied by itself, it showed no superiority in the overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā.

These observations need no further explanation. The reader is reminded that all Alif Lām Rā suras are Meccan. The special position of Al-Isrā’ has been explained on page 125. Here in the Alif Lām Rā group, sura Al-Isrā’ seems to function as a distinguishing factor between the suras which were revealed and also recorded consecutively (observation #4), and the suras which were recorded consecutively but revealed separately (observation no. 1, 2, 3, & 5).

The most important observation here is that the Alif Lām Rā suras must be combined according to their revelation sequence, regardless of their recorded sequence. We have seen that the Alif Lām Mīm suras were required to join each other according to their recorded sequence, regardless of their revelation order. This is clarified in Table 58. Therefore, the Qur’anic initials Alif

## Page 165

Lām Mīm inform us that the recorded sequence of the Qur’an is the correct, divinely inspired redording sequence. These three letters occur in two Medinan suras which are recorded consecutively, though revealed separately, and in four Meccan suras which are recorded consecutively, though revealed separately (see Table 58). The Medinan group is separated from the Meccan group, and we saw that mathematical superiority is observed only when these two groups are separated, with each group united and averaged.

On the other hand, the function of the initials Alif Lām Rā is to inform us that the revelation sequence of the Qur’an is the correct, divinely inspired, revelation sequence. We have seen that sura Al-Hijr by itself exhibits no mathematical superiority in the overall frequency of Alif Lām Rā. It had to be combined with its sister suras which preceded it directly in the chronological sequence. As shown in Table 58, in the recorded sequence of Qur’an, sura Al-Hijr (no. 15) is separated from its sister suras by sura Al-Ra‘ad (no. 13), and sura Ibrāhīm (no. 14).

## Page 170

11. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād (A.L.M.S.):**

This set of Qur’anic initials occurs in one sura, namely Al-A‘rāf (The Purgatory). This sura ranks 7 in the recorded sequence, and 39 in the revelation sequence of Qur’an. The percentage values of Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād, the absolute frequency values, and the average per verse values are shown in Tables 59, 60, and 61, respectively. Computation of the overall frequency of Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād was carried out for all the suras which contain a higher percentage than sura Al-A‘rāf. The results are shown in Table 62. Although a relatively large number of suras do have a higher percentage, only four suras are actually higher than sura Al-A‘rāf in the overall frequency of Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād. These four suras are Al-Nisā’ (Women), Al-Baqara (The Heifer), Āli-‘Imrān (The ‘Imrān Family), and Al-Mā-eda (The Table). All these are Medinan suras. It appears, therefore, that sura Al-A‘rāf is mathematically SUPERIOR TO ALL THE MECCAN SURAS in the overall frequency of Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād. We note that sura Al-A‘rāf is a Meccan sura which occurs in a predominantly Medinan section of the Qur’ān. We also note that this Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād sura is a loner with regard to this particular set of initials. Thus, just as the sura was not combined and averaged with any other sura, no combining or averaging of any suras was required to see the superiority of Al-A‘rāf over all the Meccan suras.

## Page 175

12. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Alif Lām Mīm Rā (A.L.M.R.):**

This set of initials occurs in one sura, namely, Al-Ra‘d (Thunder), which ranks 13 in the recorded sequence and 96 in the revelation sequence of Qur’ān. The percent occurrence of these letters, their absolute frequency, and their average occurrence per verse throughout the Qur’ān are shown in Tables 63, 64, and 65, respectively. When we look at the percentage figures (Table 63), we note that sura Al-Ra‘d (Thunder) has the high rank of 84 among the Qur’ān’s 114 suras. However, when we take a closer look at the suras which contain a higher percentage of Alif Lām Mīm Rā, we observe that all these suras are in fact lower in the overall frequency values. Using all three values to calculate the magnitudes of difference above and/or below sura Al-Ra‘d, we find that ALL the suras with a higher percentage bear a negative sign in front of the net difference as compared to Al-Ra‘d (see Table 66).

This observation in effect tells us that sura Al-Ra‘d is **SUPERIOR TO ALL THE SURAS WITHOUT EXCEPTION** in the overall frequency of Alif Lām Mīm Rā. We note that this set of initials occurs in a single sura, where no combining/averaging is required. Consequently, no combining/averaging of any suras was required to see the mathematical superiority of sura Al-Ra‘d. The reader will note that this has been a consistent observation throughout the study. Whenever the initials occurred in a number of suras, it was required

## Page 176

to combine/average all the suras with identical initials. On the other hand, when combining/averaging was not required for the initialed sura, no combining/averaging was required anywhere else.

Another worthwhile observation is that the initialed Meccan suras were always superior to ALL Meccan suras, while the initialed Medinan suras were found superior to ALL the suras, Meccan and Medinan. This was observed here in sura Al-Ra‘d with regard to Alif Lām Mīm Rā, and also in the two Medinan suras Al-Baqara and Āli-‘Imrān when we discussed the Alif Lām Mīm initials. The reader recalls that Alif Lām Mīm Sād which occur in the Meccan sura Al-A‘rāf were superior in that sura over ALL the Meccan suras, but not all the Medinan suras. The same observation of course holds for the seven Meccan Ha Mīm suras, the Meccan Tā Hā sura, and the Meccan Alif Lām Mīm suras. The fact that the superiority of the initialed Meccan suras was limited to the Meccan suras confirms for us the locations of revelation. And the fact that the mathematical superiority of all the initialed Medinan suras covers ALL suras of the Qur’an without exception, informs us that the details of the divine commandments which are provided in the Medinan suras consummate, complement, and perfect the initial commandments in the Meccan suras.

## Page 181

13. THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād (KHY‘AṢ):

This set of initials occurs in one Meccan sura, namely, Maryam (Mary). It ranks 19 in the recorded sequence; 44 in the revelation sequence of Qur’an. There is only one sura which bears one of the initials in common with sura Maryam, and which occurs in consecutive order after Maryam both in the recording and the revelation sequences. This one sura is Ṭā Hā, which ranks 20 in the recorded sequence; 45 in the revelation sequence.

The percentage occurrence values of Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād in each sura, the absolute frequency of occurrence values, and the average per verse values are shown in Tables 67, 68, and 69, respectively. When we look at the percentage list, we note that sura Maryam does have a high percentage of the letters Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād. Only 34 suras out of the Qur’an’s 114 contain a higher percentage of these initials. However, we note that if we take into consideration the letter Hā which occurs consecutively in suras Maryam and Ṭā Hā, we note that only 20 suras contain a higher percentage of Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād. By combining and averaging the letter Hā only of suras Maryam and Ṭā Hā, then using this average percentage of Hā to calculate the Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād percentage, the resulting value is 21.005. When we insert this value in its proper place in the percentage table, we see that only 20 suras have a higher percentage value. Thus, the overall frequency of Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād was calculated for each of the 34 suras which are

## Page 182

higher before considering the letter Hā of sura Ṭā Hā and also for the 20 suras which are higher after considering the letter Hā from sura Tā Hā. The results are shown in Tables 70 and 71, respectively. We note from Table 70 that nine suras have higher overall frequencies of Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād than sura Maryam. These suras are Yū suf (Joseph), Al-Zumur (The Throngs), Al-Anfāl (The Spoils), Al-Qaṣaṣ (History), Al-Nūr (The Light), Al-Hajj (Pilgrimage), Al-Shūrā (Consensus), Ṭā Hā (Ṭ.H.), and Muḥammad. Since these include Meccan as well as Medinan suras, we conclude that sura Maryam lacks the overall superiority when considered by itself.

It appears therefore, that we are unable to ignore the fact that the letter Hā was revealed and recorded consecutively in the two suras Maryam and Ṭā Hā. The initial Hā even occupies the same place in both suras, namely, the second spot. Very clearly, we are told here that the placement of the letter Hā in these two suras was not accidental. Once again we are shown that each alphabet in the Qur’an was carefully calculated.

By looking at Table 71, we note right away that only six suras bear the plus sign, i.e., they are higher than sura Maryam in the overall frequency of KHY‘AṢ. These six suras are Al-Qaṣaṣ, (History), Al-Nūr (The Light), Al-Hajj (Pilgrimage), Al-Shūrā (Consensus), Ṭā Hā (Ṭ.H.), and Al-Fatḥ (Victory). Thus, each contains a higher overall frequency of the initials Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād. And we note that three of these suras are Meccan, the other three are Medinan. But we also note that all

## Page 183

three Meccan suras are initialed suras.

Sura Al-Qasas (History) is initialed with Ṭā Sīn Mīm and should be combined/averaged with its sister sura. This takes it out of this comparison. Sura Al-Shūrā (Consensus) belongs to the Hā Mīm group and should be combined/averaged with its six sister suras. The remaining Meccan sura is Ṭā Hā which of course bears the letter Hā in common with sura Maryam. Thus, we see here again that when combining/averaging is required of the initialed suras, the same combining/averaging is required of the suras being compared.

The observation to be recorded here is that, when the letter Hā of both Maryam and Ṭā Hā is taken into consideration, sura Maryam is SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS in the overall frequency of Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād. The reader is reminded that both suras Maryam and Ṭā Hā are Meccan. This observation is consistent with the other initialed Meccan suras as discussed on page 162; a fact that confirms the location of revelation of the Qur’anic chapters.

## Page 189

14. **THE QUR’ANIC INITIALS** Hā Mīm ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf (H.M. ‘A.S.Q.):

This set of initials occurs in one sura, namely, Al-Shūrā (Consensus). It ranks 42 in the recorded sequence; 62 in the revelation sequence of the Qur’an. The first two letters, Hā Mīm, form an independent verse. In that respect, sura Al-Shūrā belongs to the Hā Mīm group of seven suras. The three letters ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf constitute verse 2 of sura Al-Shūrā. The percentage occurrence of these initials, their absolute frequency in each sura, and their average occurrence in each verse of each sura are listed in Tables 72, 73, and 74, respectively. The reader by now is familiar with the types of computations and the bases for finding out the overall frequency value for a given set of Qur’anic initials. When these computations were carried out for the five letters Hā Mīm ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf, it was observed that sura Al-Shūrā exhibits no mathematical superiority. As a matter of fact, 18 suras were found superior to sura Al-Shūrā in the overall frequency of Hā Mīm ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf. Fourteen of these suras were Meccan, the other 4 Medinan. Of the 14 superior Meccan suras, 8 were initialed suras, and 6 were regular, non-initialed suras. Therefore, when considered by itself, sura Al-Shūrā cannot claim any mathematical superiority with regard to its own initials. It appears, therefore, that we cannot ignore the sister suras of Al-Shūrā (the other 6 Hā Mīm suras).

## Page 190

This lack of mathematical superiority was also observed when sura Al-Shūra was considered by itself with regard to the initials Hā Mīm, and also with regard to the initials ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf. When the average Hā Mīm of the seven Hā Mīm suras was added to the ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf values of sura Al-Shūra, the computer data again showed lack of superiority. Finally, the values of ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf in the seven Hā Mīm suras were combined together and averaged in the usual manner. The average of ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf was then added to the one Hā Mīm of sura Al-Shūra to represent the Qur’anic initials Hā Mīm‘Ayn Sīn Qāf. This is the ONLY combination where mathematical superiority was observed. Results of computing the overall frequency values are shown in Table 75, and the attempted combinations, with their results, are shown in Table 76. From Table 75, we can see that only 3 suras have the plus sign, indicating their superiority over the Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf average of the seven suras. These 3 superior suras are Hūd (Prophet Hūd), Al-Rūm (The Romans), and Al-Fatḥ (Victory). Strikingly, the only other two sets of Qur’anic initials which occur in consecutive suras, namely, Alif Lām Rā and Alif Lām Mīm, are represented here. Thus, sura Hūd represents the Alif Lām Rā group, and sura Al-Rūm represents the Alif Lām Mīm group. After combining these two suras with their respective sister suras, only one sura is left which exhibits superiority over the Hā Mīm group with regard to the overall frequency of Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf. This one sura, Al-Fatḥ, is a Medinan sura.

## Page 191

Therefore, when the seven Hā Mīm suras are taken into consideration with regard to the ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf initials, the resulting Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf in them is SUPERIOR TO ALL MECCAN SURAS. The reader is reminded here that this is the same conclusion observed for the initials Hā Mīm in the seven Hā Mīm suras (page 114).

Similarly, if we disregard the average per verse values, and rely only on the percentage values and absolute frequency values in our computations, we find that sura Al-Shūra is SUPERIOR TO ALL SURAS OF THE QUR’AN WITHOUT EXCEPTION with regard to the initials Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf (of course the ‘Ayn Sīn Qāf of the sister Hā Mīm suras must be considered as pointed out above). This observation, too, was noted for the initials Hā Mīm (page 114).

## Page 197

Chapter 7

**SUMMARY AND CONCLUSION**

The Qur’anic initials and the major observations pertaining to them are all listed in Table 77. By glancing through this table, the reader notes that each one of the Qur’anic initials has its own characteristics. Each one serves to reassure man with regard to specific aspects of the Qur’an (such as the sequence of recording, the sequence of revelation, the length of the verses, the length of the suras, the location of revelation, etc.). Thus, all aspects of the Qur’an are proven and confirmed. These aspects include:

1. **The Qur’an is the word of God:**

The Qur’anic initials as a whole have shown us that every word, indeed every letter, in the Qur’an was carefully calculated. The Qur’an itself states this fact very clearly in the first verse of sura Hūd, which incidentally includes the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Rā. Placement of the Qur’anic initials in their specific locations proves the existence of advance knowledge of the distribution pattern of the alphabet throughout the Qur’an. No one can claim that such advance knowledge is attainable by man; any man.

To translate this into a physical, tangible evidence, the computer was asked to calculate the number of manipulations one should master in order to write a mathematically-controlled book such as the Qur’an. The composition of Qur’an involves 114 chapters where 14 alphabet letters were distributed according to specific

## Page 198

combinations. According to the well-known mathematical formula, the number of manipulations involved in this case equals 114^14. The computer’s answer was that 114^14 = 6.26 x 10^26 is the number of manipulations that must be controlled in order to write a book like the Qur’an. This value, 626,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000 (626 septillions) is certainly beyond the capacity of any creature, including today’s most sophisticated computers. When we look at this number, we can readily appreciate the divine statement in sura Al-Isra’ (The Night Journey), verse 88 (Say, if all the humans and all the Jinns banded together in order to produce a like this, they will surely fail

![Arabic Text](وَلَوْ جَمِعْنَا الْإِنسَ وَالْجِنَّ عَلَى أَنْ يَأْتُوا بِمِثْلِ هَذَا الْقُرْآنِ لَا جَاءُوا بِمِثْلِهِ وَلَوْ كَانُوا إِحْضَرُونَ بَعْضَهُ)

Furthermore, this is the number of controlled manipulations for a specific distribution pattern of the alphabet, without placing them in useful sentences. To place these mathematically distributed alphabetic letters in useful sentences is another complete job. This fact, and the full awareness of the two requirements, is indicated at the beginning of sura Hūd. The first verse in this sura says, “Alif Lām Rā. This is a book whose verses have been perfectly designed THEN ELUCIDATED. It comes from a Wise, Omniscient (God).”

![Arabic Text](الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ رَبِّ الْعَالَمِينَ)

Thus, this verse tells us of the two jobs required; the job of distributing the alphabet in a perfectly controlled and designed pattern, AND the job of making from these letters meaningful sentences. The reader can easily visualize the awesomeness of both jobs.

## Page 201

Since the Qur’an is proven to be the word of God, using the ultimate in scientific proof (namely, mathematics), it serves to confirm the miracles of all previous prophets. Thus, the perpetual miracle of Muhammad puts in our hands physical, examinable evidence that Moses did indeed split the sea with his staff. Though we never saw the staff of Moses turn into a snake, we now have physical proof that such a miracle took place indeed. Since every word in the Qur’an is mathematically proven to have come from God, we now know for sure that Jesus Christ did indeed revive the dead. In summary, by proving that the Qur’an is God’s undisputable revelation, all the Qur’anic miracles of previous prophets and messengers are equally proven.

2. **The present order of recording the Qur’anic chapters is the divinely inspired order:**

This is confirmed by the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm (A.L.M.), where we were required to group the Alif Lām Mīm suras according to their recorded sequence, regardless of their revelation sequence.

3. **The revelation order of the Qur’anic chapters, as we know it today is the divinely dictated sequence:**

This aspect of Qur’an is confirmed by the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Rā (A.L.R.). As the reader recalls, the suras initialed with these letters had to be grouped together according to their revelation sequence, regardless of their recorded sequence.

4. **The locations of revelation of the Qur’anic chapters, whether Meccan or Medinan, are confirmed:**

## Page 202

The Qur'anic initials involved here include, Ṭā Hā, Hā Mīm, Alif Lām Mīm, Alif Lām Mīm Ṣād, Kāf Hā Yā ‘Ayn Ṣād, and Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf (see Table 77).

5. **The particular way of spelling certain words in the Qur’an is divinely dictated:**

A well known fact is that the Qur'anic Arabic is unique. It is not identical to conventional Arabic. For example the word “salat” is written as “salawah” ![salawah](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=سَلَوَات) instead of ![salat](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=صَلَات), the word “zakat” is written as “zakawah” ![zakawah](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=زَكَاة) instead of ![zakat](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=زَكَاة), the word “hayat (life)” is written as “Hayawah” ![hayawah](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=حَيَاة) instead of ![hayat](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=حَيَاة). Many other words are written in a unique way in the Qur’an, such as ![murabaha](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=مُرَابَحَة) ![murabaha](https://via.placeholder.com/15/000000/000000?text=مُرَابَحَة), etc. Needless to say, changing the Qur'anic Arabic into conventional Arabic will upset all the mathematical characteristics of the Qur’an’s alphabet, and will render the Qur'anic initials totally meaningless and insignificant. Only recently, there have been demands to change the Qur’an’s Arabic into conventional Arabic in order to “make the Qur’an easier.” Such demands were quite frequent in the past five years, especially in Egypt. The findings of this study, therefore, are quite timely. They inform us that the specific way of writing the Qur’an should never be altered in any way.

6. **The Qur’an’s specific way of dividing each sura into verses is divinely prescribed:**

The lengths of verses as they came to us must never be tampered with. A good example should be pointed out here. When ‘Abdullah Yusuf ‘Ali translated the

## Page 203

meanings of the Qur’an, he arbitrarily (and I am sure with good and sincere intentions) changed the lengths of certain verses “because they sounded better to him.” An example of this is verse #3 of sura #3 (Āli-‘Imrān). The verse came to us with the ending at the word “Al-Injīl.” But ‘Abdullah Yusuf ‘Ali changed the position of ending the verse to the word “Al-Furqān” of verse #4. In view of this study, this type of alteration is not permissible. As we have seen from the Qur’anic initials Hā Mīm and Hā Mīm-‘Ayn Sīn Qāf, the average per verse value is a highly significant property throughout the Qur’an. When the average per verse values were considered, the suras containing these initials were superior to all the Meccan suras, and not to all the Medinan suras. And when these values were disregarded, the superiority of these suras covered all suras of the Qur’an without exception.

7. The opening statement “Bismillāhi-r-Rahmāni-r-Rahīm (In the Name of God, the Compassionate, the Merciful) is an integral part of each sura, except sura #9 (Al-Tawba). This conclusion is based on three observations:

i - This opening statement is an independent verse in the opening sura, Al-Fātiḥa.
ii - The last sura in the Qur’an (sura Al-Nās) contains 99 letters, same number as God’s magnificent names or attributes. It is the ONLY sura which contains this number of alphabets. The number 99 does include the letters of “Bismillāhi-r-Rahmāni-r-Rahīm.”
iii - Sura #9 (Al-Tawba) deliberately omits this opening statement.

## Page 204

It must be repeated here that this study merely scratches the surface; unveils the tip of a huge iceberg. Undoubtedly, many Muslims who are better versed in mathematics will deduce many more conclusions from the data presented here. During the course of this work many side phenomena were observed. Pursuing any one of those phenomena would have distracted the worker from any specific path, and would easily take the life time of many workers to reach a full and complete understanding. It is for that reason that all the raw data are included in this book.

Finally, one cannot help but observe that exactly 14 centuries have passed before the uncovering of the mathematical properties of these 14 sets of Qur’anic initials, which are composed of 14 letters. This year, 1393 AH-1973 AD, marks 14 lunar centuries from the time of the first revelation of Qur’an, and 14 solar centuries from the time of Muḥammad’s birth. There is even a special significance of the number 14 in the older scriptures. In the gospel of Matthew, Chapter 1 verse 17, it says, “Thus, the total number of generations is: from Abraham to David, 14 generations; from David to the Babylonian captivity, 14 generations; from the Babylonian captivity to the Messiah, 14 generations.”

A natural question then would be, “Why did God Almighty choose to conceal the significance of the 14 Qur’anic initials for 14 centuries?” In the author’s humble opinion, this deliberate concealment is correlated to the manner in which the Qur’an was revealed. Thus.

## Page 205

we have here a book which was written over a period of 23 years. Its individual verses came down scattered, i.e., widely separated from each other both in time and place. Some Meccan suras include Medinan verses, and some Medinan suras include Meccan verses. The sequence of recording the Qur’anic chapters does not follow any particular system. The recorded sequence is totally unrelated to the chronological sequence of revelation. This seemingly confused manner has, in turn, confused the enemies of the Qur’an. When the prejudiced critics of the Qur’an found no scientific contradictions in the Qur’an, they rejected the Qur’an as the word of God on the basis that “it was written haphazardly.” When they failed to detect any contradictions in the Qur’an, they attacked the Qur’an’s method of revelation and recording. During the past 14 centuries, the enemies of Islam settled for the conclusion that a “randomly written” book like that could not be the word of God. Now, after those critics settled for this particular conclusion, people can find out for themselves, through this book, that every word in the Qur’an, indeed every letter, was carefully calculated and perfectly designed. The obvious question to such critics is “Now, what do you have to say? On what basis can you now reject the Qur’an as God’s message to humanity?”

As it turns out, God Almighty did provide us with strong hints as to the possible significance of the Qur’anic initials. Thus, wherever the Qur’anic initials occur, they are immediately followed by a statement that it is God

## Page 206

who revealed the Qur’an. For example, the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm of sura Al-Baqara are followed by the statement, “This book cannot be doubted.” The Alif Lām Mīm in the next sura, Āli-‘Imrān, are followed by the statement, “Allah, the one and only God; the Living; the Eternal God, is the One who revealed this book to you (O Muḥammad).” The next three suras Al-Nisā’, Al-Mā-eda, and Al-An‘ām, do not have any Qur’anic initials. These three suras lack the opening statement about the revelation of Qur’an from God. Then comes sura Al-A‘rāf. This sura does have Qur’anic initials. The first two verses of this sura say, “A.L.M.Ṣ This book is being revealed to you (O Muḥammad) and you shall have no doubts about it. You shall warn therewith. It is a message to the believers.” Thus throughout the Qur’an, we observe this kind of statement immediately following each one of the Qur’anic initials. The only exceptions to this rule occur in suras Al-‘Ankabūt (no. 29) and Al-Rūm (no. 30). However, as the reader recalls, these two suras belong to the Meccan group of suras which are initialed with Alif Lām Mīm. The first revealed sura in this group is Luqmān. Sura Luqmān starts with the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm, followed by the characteristic statement about the Qur’an’s perfect composition.

Finally, the reader can easily appreciate the miracle of the Qur’an by reflecting on the following simplified example:

Suppose you are asked to write a book of 10 unequal

## Page 207

chapters, on condition that the first chapter must contain the highest overall frequency of the letter A. You will probably carry out this task without much effort. All you will have to do is to make use of the letter A more often in the first chapter than any other chapter. Now, let us add one more condition. In addition to the high relative frequency of the letter A in the first chapter, you are required to show the highest relative frequency of the letters B and C in chapter 2. You can easily see that the task of writing the book would be more difficult. You may still be able to write the book, but now you have to control the distribution of the three letters A, B, and C. You have to choose words containing the letter A more frequently as you compose chapter 1, and you have to utilize words containing the letters B and C more frequently as you compose chapter 2. Now let us add the condition that the first three chapters must contain a higher percentage of the letter D than chapter 4, and the last three chapters must contain a lower percentage of D than chapter 7. You can see that the task is already becoming impossible. You have to choose certain words, utilizing certain alphabetic letters, in order to satisfy the requirements mentioned. You have to put more A’s in the first chapter, more B’s and C’s in the second chapter, and you have to keep track of the frequency and proportions of the letter D throughout the book. And let us not forget that you have to put those mathematically controlled words in useful, comprehensible, sentences. Not only meaningful sentences, but also sentences which

## Page 208

are linguistically and grammatically perfect.

This example illustrates for us that even after knowing the mathematical rules of composing the Qur’an, no human being in the past, present, or future can compose a similar work. We have been told by the computer that one has to master 626,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000 combinations of alphabetic letters in order to produce the mathematical distribution of the Qur’an’s alphabet. Composing useful sentences from these letters is an additional task.

It is, therefore, no wonder that God tells us in His message:

![Arabic Text](وَلَئِنْ جَعَلْنَاهُ قُرْآنًا أَعْجَمِيًّا لَّقَالُوا إِنَّمَا أَخْتَلَطَهُ بَعْضُهُ وَإِنْ كُنَّا لَمِنَ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ)

If we reveal this Qur’an to a mountain, you would see it humbled and cracked from the glory of God (59:21)

And we now know for sure that:

![Arabic Text](قُلْ لَوْ جَمِعْنَا بِهِ جِنَّةً وَإِنْسًا ثُمَّ أَتَوْا بِمِثْلِهِ قُرْآنًا مُتَّفَاقًا لَّقَدْ سَبَقَهُ مِنَّا قُرْآنٌ وَصَلَاةٌ مُؤْمِنُونَ)

"If all the humans, and all the jinns, banded together in order to produce a Qur’an like this, they will surely fail, no matter how much assistance they may lend one another. (17:88)."

## Page 209

Addendum

An investigation into the significance of the Qur’anic initials would be incomplete without referring to what the Prophet Muhammad said about them. The following account is well documented in the reliable sources of Hadīth and Qur’anic studies, including Al-Baydāwy’s commentary and Al-Suyūṭy’s classic reference, “Al-Itqān Fī ‘Ulūm Al-Qur’an.” A group of Jewish scripturists were discussing the Qur’an with the Prophet. When he recited the first few verses of sura Al-Baqara (The Heifer) to them, they said, “How do you expect us to believe in a religion which is supposed to survive 71 years only?” This was an obvious reference to the numerical value they assigned to the Qur’anic initials Alif Lām Mīm (ALM) which make up the first verse of sura Al-Baqara.

The numerical value of the letter A, according to the ancient science of gametria, is 1, the numerical value of the letter L is 30, and the value of the letter M is 40. When added up, the numerical value of ALM is 71. The significant incident in that confrontation between the Prophet and the Jewish scripturists, is that he did not object to their method of assigning numerical values to ALM, and using the product as a measure of Islam’s life-time. The Prophet’s answer was, “But you forget that there is also ALMS, ALR, ALMR.” The lack of objection on the part of the Prophet to assigning numerical values to the Qur’anic initials is quite significant. Thus, the numerical values of all the Qur’anic initials were calculated. The results are shown in the

## Page 210

Table below. The grand total of these calculations is 4949. If the inference is indeed correct, from the confrontation incident between the Prophet and the Jewish scripturists, this number (4949 years) may indicate the life-time of Islam.
